title: Statistics APIs

  order: 10
---

Use this application programming interface (API) reference to find the statistics and analytics available for your F5 Distributed Cloud resources. The tables cover specification-defined queries, related discovery, and retrieval of existing reports. Feature entitlement, resource configuration, access, and telemetry determine which queries return data in your account.

| Resource group | Available information | Required resource or feature |
| --- | --- | --- |
| [Application traffic, origins, and service health](#application-traffic-origins-and-service-health) | Traffic rates, errors, latency, throughput, connections, service graphs, and health scores | Load balancer, origin, discovered service, or service mesh with telemetry |
| [API activity, discovery, and security analytics](#api-activity-discovery-and-security-analytics) | Endpoint inventory, calls, activity rankings, sensitive data, schemas, and vulnerabilities | API discovery or imported API definitions on a virtual host |
| [WAF and application security](#waf-and-application-security) | Request and rule-hit counters, events, incidents, suspicious users, and security evidence | Web application firewall (WAF) or application security feature |
| [Bot defense](#bot-defense) | Traffic classification, actions, attacked endpoints, automation, known bots, and peer comparisons | Bot Defense protected application and reporting entitlement |
| [Client-side defense](#client-side-defense) | Scripts, behaviors, form fields, network interactions, affected users, and summaries | Client-Side Defense protected browser application |
| [Device and data intelligence](#device-and-data-intelligence) | Device risk, transaction trends, linked accounts, device history, and data summaries | Device Intelligence, Data Intelligence, or Application Traffic Insights |
| [DDoS protection](#ddos-protection) | Traffic, top talkers, attacks, alerts, events, mitigations, and transit use | Distributed denial of service (DDoS) protection and protected network |
| [DNS](#dns) | Query metrics and logs, load balancer/pool health, and health changes | Domain Name System (DNS) zone or DNS load balancer |
| [CDN](#cdn) | Traffic, cache metrics, cacheability, access logs, and operation status | Content delivery network (CDN) distribution or caching-enabled load balancer |
| [Sites, connectivity, cloud networking, and policy counters](#sites-connectivity-cloud-networking-and-policy-counters) | Site/node metrics, topology, flows, cloud-connect bytes, policy hits, and session decisions | Connected sites, cloud networks, policy evaluation, or flow collection |
| [Kubernetes, workloads, and storage](#kubernetes-workloads-and-storage) | Workload use, packet/byte metrics, storage capacity, inventory, audit logs, and events | Site Kubernetes cluster or virtual Kubernetes workload |
| [Logs, audit activity, alerts, and platform events](#logs-audit-activity-alerts-and-platform-events) | Access/firewall logs, audit records, alerts, login events, platform activity, and log-delivery status | Resource logging and access to the relevant namespace or tenant |
| [Synthetic monitoring](#synthetic-monitoring) | Monitor health, latency, regional history, events, certificates, and security reports | Configured synthetic HTTP or DNS monitor |
| [Billing, consumption, and quota usage](#billing-consumption-and-quota-usage) | Feature usage, consumption, resource limits, and current quota use | Billing, Bot Defense reporting, or quota access |

## Shared query concepts

Authenticate against your tenant API base URL using an API token with permission for the requested operation. For the shared token header and environment setup, use [Overview](../#set-up-curl); keep credentials private. Send a JSON request body for POST queries. The method is part of each contract: POST can retrieve analytics without changing a resource.

Replace path parameters such as `{namespace}`, `{name}`, `{site}`, and `{network_id}` with the identifiers of
the selected resource. The paths below are complete API paths relative to the tenant base URL. [Query
reference](../setup/#read-the-load-balancer-identifiers) shows how to discover load balancer and virtual host
identities. A configuration object name, graph node identity, API endpoint path, and site name serve different
purposes; use the identifier required by the linked request schema.

| Query concept | How to use it |
| --- | --- |
| Namespace and tenant access | A namespace query normally selects that namespace. Use tenant-wide queries only when the operation documents them and your account has access. A literal `system` path, or a row marked `system` only, requires that reserved namespace. Some DDoS and site APIs use `{namespace}` in the path but accept only `system`. |
| Time window | Set the endpoint's start and end fields explicitly. Many metrics and log APIs accept Unix timestamps or RFC 3339 timestamps; other contracts use `timeRange`, `timestamp`, `period_in_days`, or nested time filters. Consult the schema for defaults and supported formats. |
| Resolution and rollups | `step` or `step_size` controls point spacing. `range` controls the lookback used to evaluate each point. They serve different purposes. Stored rollups can increase the effective step; use the returned step and timestamps. [Load balancer metrics](../service-graph/#baseline-and-later-rollup) shows the measured effect. |
| Metric selection | `field_selector`, `metric_selector`, or `metrics` selects returned measurements. Graph APIs separate node and edge metrics and health scores. Select a meaningful family and supported dimensions; the schema defines allowed combinations. |
| Filtering | Contracts use different selectors: `label_filter`, a matcher string `filter`, structured `filters`, or log `query` expressions. These are endpoint-specific formats. A virtual host filter accepted by one API does not automatically apply to another. |
| Grouping and aggregation | `group_by` determines dimensions; `aggs` requests named log/event aggregations. A top-N query returns a ranking, not every matching record. Choose supported grouping labels and aggregation types from the request schema. |
| Pagination | Follow the endpoint's cursor, scroll, search-after, offset, or page contract. Several log and event APIs cap a batch at 500 records. An empty returned `scroll_id` ends scrolling. Compare collected records with `total_hits` where supplied; a first page is not a complete window. |
| Responses and empty data | Inspect the successful response schema, units, labels, timestamps, and returned resolution. HTTP 200 with no series means the selected window has no returned points. It does not establish zero traffic or populated telemetry. |

### Metric families and dimensions

The families below describe the measurements you can select. The endpoint tables link both request and successful response schemas for complete fields, selector rules, enum values, and response shapes.

| Family | Measurements and principal dimensions |
| --- | --- |
| Service graphs | HTTP rates, errors, latency, throughput, origin traffic, round-trip time (RTT), connections, and Transmission Control Protocol (TCP) metrics. Group by namespace, site, application type, service, virtual host, virtual host type, virtual IP address (VIP), node, or cacheability as supported. Health scores cover connectivity, performance, security, reliability, and overall health. |
| Graph DDoS metrics | Node DDoS selectors expose detected and mitigated packet and bit rates. Use `VHOST` or `NAMESPACE` with `VHOST` grouping where the schema requires it. `NEW_CONNECTION_RATE` does not support a service filter. See the [metric-family guidance](../service-graph/). |
| Application security and WAF | Request, event, and client/server rule-hit counters. Dimensions include virtual host, event type, site, service, instance, rule identifier/severity/tag, firewall mode, and bot classification, depending on the endpoint. |
| Bot reporting and intelligence | Transaction trends by traffic type, action, application, endpoint, attack intent, geography, user agent, and autonomous system number (ASN). Device queries add risk score, confidence, linked account distribution, signals, and high-risk transactions. Versioned routes can change classification and filter support. |
| DNS and CDN | DNS metrics group by zone, domain, country, query type, response code, client subnet, or answers. CDN selectors cover traffic and cache behavior by distribution, cache hit type, country, HTTP/TLS version, and point of presence. Transport Layer Security (TLS) monitor queries add protocol, grade, and certificate expiration information. |
| Connectivity and sites | Reachability, connection status, latency, throughput, drops, deployments, pods, flows, and node CPU/memory/disk use. Dimensions include site, node, interface, network/connectivity type, source/destination site, segment, and cloud connect. Cloud-connect selectors expose inbound, outbound, and total bytes. |
| Policies and sessions | Hit counters and policy-evaluation latency; session decisions include allowed, denied, and evaluation timeout. Group by the labels documented for the policy, namespace, site, service, or virtual server. |
| Kubernetes and storage | Workload CPU/memory and disk reads/writes by container, pod, and site; persistent volume claim (PVC) capacity/use by PVC and site. Site pod and virtual machine instance metrics expose inbound/outbound packets, bytes, and dropped packets. |
| Billing and quota | Feature/stock keeping unit (SKU) usage details and summaries, Bot Defense transaction consumption, and resource quota limits/current use. Availability and time granularity follow the billing or reporting contract. |

## Measured guides and current empty series

For measured queries in this deployment, use [HTTP access logs](../access-logs/), [Load balancer
metrics](../service-graph/), [Origin performance](../origin-performance/), [Application
health](../application-health/), [API activity](../api-discovery/), [WAF metrics](../firewall-metrics/), and
[Security events](../security-events/). Other resource families below describe specification-defined
capabilities and need their own resources and verification before you can treat them as measured examples.

| Surface | Current evidence | Next step |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Graph `ACTIVE_CONNECTIONS` and `NEW_CONNECTION_RATE` | Requested with VHOST traffic metrics; no returned series in the measured window | Capture meaningful connection telemetry before adding a populated example |
| `/api/data/namespaces/{namespace}/app_security/metrics` | Namespace and `VH_NAME` query returned HTTP 200 with empty counters | Retain WAF counters and authoritative event logs; capture meaningful metric points before presenting counts |

These are unavailable telemetry for the measured selector and window. HTTP 200 does not make an empty response a populated result. See [Security events](../security-events/#empty-security-event-metric-response) for timestamps and the empty security-metric response.

## Reading the endpoint tables

Each method/path appears once under its primary resource group. Statistics and analytics queries retrieve
measurements, logs, events, or analytical summaries. Related discovery and status queries help identify
resources, supported data, and current state. Existing reports and artifacts retrieve previously available
output. Configuration creation/replacement/deletion, enable/disable operations, credential retrieval, traffic
generation, and report-generation requests are outside this reference.

The operation link explains the query, and the schema links define its request and successful response. Routes
marked **corrected source** are documented in the corrected specifications but absent from the merged enriched
specification. The three cloud-connect metric queries are retained with direct source links. A newer version
does not imply deprecation of an older route; a deprecation label appears only when the source declares it.

For API endpoint paths ending in `/pdf`, the response is a **probability density function** of sizes or latency, not a PDF document download. Report download routes are listed separately.

## API activity, discovery, and security analytics

### API activity, discovery, and security analytics: statistics and analytics queries
